The Ministers for Justice, Finance, Development and Investment, National Defence, Education and Religious Affairs, Labour and Social Affairs, Home, Migration and Asylum, State, as well as the Deputy Ministers of Finance, Foreign and Home Affairs submitted a draft law on 29.11.2021: "Reforms in the legislative framework of the National School of Justice and other urgent provisions of the Ministry of Justice". Reference shall be made to the competent continuous committee. Ladies and gentlemen, we are entering the agenda of the LEGISLATIVE WORK The only debate and vote on the principle, articles, amendments and the whole draft law of the Ministry of Development and Investment: "Mastery investment and improvement of the investment environment by speeding up procedures in private and strategic investment and setting up a framework for craft companies". The Conference of Presidents decided at its meeting on 25 November to discuss the draft law at a single sitting on the principle of articles and amendments. The speakers will be registered electronically until the end of the speech by the second rapporteur. Is there any objection to this basic procedural proposal?
ParlaMint-GR_2021-11-30-S1-commons.u2	The House unanimously agreed. The new Republic's rapporteur, Christos Tarantilis, has been called to Step for 15 minutes. Please open the electronic record system. Mr. Tarantil, you have the floor.
ParlaMint-GR_2021-11-30-S1-commons.u3	Thank you, Mr President. Minister, ladies and gentlemen, the draft law of the Ministry of Development and Investment which we are about to vote on today is an important initiative by the government, which tends to create an institutional framework that suits us in a modern and environmentally friendly environment. Modifications proposed by this draft law are structured in three parts: Part one. Create a single framework for strategic investment by consolidating, reforming and improving the existing framework. Part two. Reform of the existing regulatory framework under Development Law 4399. Improve the functioning of individual issues of previous development laws and improve the framework for the establishment of foreign companies in Greece. Part three. Specific arrangements for the establishment and operation of devices, i.e. spin offs, are commonly called internationally. In this order, I shall refer to the principles and the specific content of each chapter. The proposed regulations in the first part of the draft law seek to reform, improve and, in particular, to integrate into a single legislative text the existing regulatory framework for strategic investment, i.e. the two main legislations on strategic investment. 4608 and 4608 The Commission's proposal is based on the principle of equal treatment of men and women in matters of social security and equal treatment. Such pluralism often led to confusion and legal uncertainty both in the candidate strategic investors and in the public administration, which is ultimately called upon to apply all the existing provisions in the course of the implementation of an investment. In fact, in these circumstances, there has been no uniform law on strategic investment, which is a reference point for anyone who wishes to choose our country as an investment destination. The first part, therefore, contains a series of arrangements which relate to all aspects that form a strategic investment, with the aim of coexistence in a law, that is, the most sensible one. The proposed arrangements shall start with the conceptual identification of the categories of strategic investment, providing subsequently for rapid authorisation, authorisation, implementation and judicial resolution of the disputes in the context of the implementation of the investments. The approach followed by the arrangements attempts to bring together individual administrative phases of approval and authorisation to specific bodies, in order to avoid bureaucratic inconveniences of the public administration and the confusion of the diverse competences between the services, in particular in complex and timely issues such as the spatial and urban status of the hosting areas. This concentration is complemented by the addition of accelerators of the licensing process, such as the use of certified assessors of the Ministry of the Environment and Energy register. The definition and categorisation of the strategic investments developed in the first articles of the bill maintains similar logic to the existing law, but with the new regulations the categories of investment strategies are enriched, while changes are made to the conditions for the integration of investment projects, in particular with a view to extending the limits of subordination, in order to make the framework more attractive and more efficient. These aims are pursued through a reduction in the entry budget for ‘State Investment 1’ from 100 to 70 million euros without any other condition or to 40 million euros at the same time as an obligation to set up seventy-five annual units of work. Subsequently, discrimination according to the field of activity is adopted in Strategic Investment 2. In other words, for investments in modern research and innovation, biotechnology, waste and waste management, agrifood, robotic and artificial intelligence, medical tourism, space, the provision of cloud computing services, the budget line goes back to 20 million without any other condition, while for all other sectors it is maintained at 30 million, together with the obligation to create fifty annual units of work. In this context, I would like to reiterate the concern of this draft law specifically for the strategic investments that will be completed in the areas covered by the territorial fair transition plans, where budget indicators are subject to a reduced rate of 25%, with a view to further strengthening the attraction of investment in these areas. In the same direction, this draft law largely reshapes the category of emblematic investments of exceptional importance. This category includes investments implemented by distinguished legal entities, including, in particular, those promoting research and innovation, the green economy and technology and significantly strengthen the Greek economy and its international competitiveness. These investments for which a specific budget limit is not provided, if they wish to benefit from the incentives provided by the Act, are linked to the resources and financial conditions of the Recovery Fund and must be completed by 31 December 2025. In other words, they are forced into forward growth and rapid results. The designation of a project as a flagship investment is assigned to a three-member committee composed of scientists of a well-known variety of specialities, established and operated by a joint decision of the Ministers for Development and Investment and Finance. The incentives provided for in this Legislation are distinguished from incentives for placement, tax incentives, incentives for rapid licensing and incentives for aid for investment strategies. Specifically and exclusively for flagrant investments, cost aid incentives are enriched further and supported in order to take the form of a grant, a leasing subsidy for equipment and a grant for employment created. With Part Two of this draft law now attempts interventions aimed at strengthening the aid schemes of the development law by adding new categories of eligible activities and accelerated interventions, as well as updating the rules concerning the establishment of foreign companies in Greece. With regard to the speeding up of procedures, the responsibility of the projects hosts is changed in such a way that the development plans of the regions of the country accept the projects of up to 1 million, from the limit of 3 million to the present time, while for all the others the responsibility is transferred centrally to the Directorate-General for Private Investment of the Ministry of Development and Investment. The aim of this regulation is to unload the regions' addresses from a significant volume of investment projects and to focus on investment projects which are truly crucial and directly crucial for local entrepreneurship and local development. Accordingly, the assessment plan shall include sworn auditors with a view to a faster examination of the eligibility of the investment projects, while at the same time establishing a time for the adoption of administrative decisions on the certification of interim implementation steps which may not be more than 30 days from the submission of the relevant audit reports and attestations. Finally, as regards the provisions of the law on tax incentives and the obligations of foreign companies established in Greece, among other things, the possibility of granting a temporary residence permit for foreign companies for the first time to carry out the preparatory actions required before tax and other authorities and to immediately commence their activities. That is, in other words, a speeding up of their installation until the final licence is issued, a process which can now take from one to a half a year and a half. The third and final part of the draft proposed legislation establishes for the first time uniform arrangements for the establishment and operation of companies of technicians which are one of the most important tools on the road towards the business exploitation of the research. Ladies and gentlemen, Greece has high-trained scientific capacity and produces global and impact research, which is confirmed by the research indicators and recent scientific studies. It is directly linked to the business exploitation of the research that is the technological, corporate types of capital types established by the research staff of public universities and research centres, for the purpose of exploiting the results and knowledge generated by them. These are special-purpose companies that differ from the common corporate forms as they use a research product produced at public universities and research centres. Therefore, the way in which they are to participate must reconcile and balance the interests of researchers, in terms of the fruits of their work and their research, with the involvement of the institutions in the business exploitation of the results produced within their walls, using their resources. The number of technicians established to date in the country is in no way proportional to the quality of the research produced, as well as to the overall performance of the business use of the research results. This delay is due to a large extent to the total absence of a uniform and relevant legislative framework which defines both the process of setting up technocratic companies and the scope for action by researchers and research organisations within them. The current framework for technobularies is fragmented in a wide range of legislation, which is extremely unclear and incomplete, leaving on the substance the critical issues of the economic nature and the operational situation of researchers to be addressed. This lack led the company to be degraded to a legal entity without a substantial identity, operating under an invisible and inactive institutional cloak. The reform sought by Part Three does not introduce new terms and rules, but it is precisely in the systeming of clear rules for the establishment and operation of technicians' companies to determine, on the one hand, the competence of the approval body and the deadline for the completion of the approval procedure, on the other hand, all stakeholders to understand the necessary, i.e. the process of establishing, the rules for the participation of researchers and research organisations or third party investors in their equity and the procedure for allocating rights and obligations to the intellectual ownership of the research results. Systematicisation and clarification of the operation of technocratic companies will make the process of establishing less bureaucratic and ultimately more attractive, contributing decisively to the provision of another incentive to researchers. The proposed regulation concerns primarily the research organisations in the country and researchers, even from different research organisations. However, it also reflects potential investors who want to invest in these companies. On this basis, henceforth the advantage of public institutions' research will be made more tangible and its benefits towards society are multiplier and visible. And that, let me tell you, is a vote of confidence in public research, public institutions and their people. Ladies and gentlemen, this draft law sets out a wide range of arrangements aimed at a modern and effective investment strategy. The pillars of the proposed bill are the basis on which the country's economic development is being opened for the years to come. The principles and arrangements analysed are the necessary treaty for a sound and long-term investment perspective. That is why I invite you to vote in favour of it. Thank you very much.

Your legislative coin, gentlemen of the government, is not only unrealistic, it is also weak in terms of what it is supposed to serve. For the first time, a number of aid schemes are presented in the bill. We have asked for clarification as to whether these will work in isolation or at the same time as you do not give clear timetables, nor is there any provision made for the depletion of resources in any of them. But we haven't got a reply so far. But also the 15-year margin - if possible! - for the implementation of a strategic investment strategy, we think it is excessive considering that to build the "Eleftherios Venizelos" airport it has only taken four years. Furthermore, the provisions you bring do not strengthen the local production footprint and the relevant multiplier effects, since Article 15 does not provide for the strengthening of the domestic production base, which is specifically demanded and required in this economic and social context. As there is no balanced regional development, nor is there any criterion for allocating investment to regions which are lagging behind in relation to the necessary infrastructure such as transport, telecommunications and logistics. But, above all, ladies and gentlemen, there is absolutely nothing for small and medium-sized enterprises or to facilitate them if their plan is adopted. And if we are currently being viewed by professionals and businessmen who have applied and been subject to previous provisions of development bills, they are well aware of the difficulty they face when they go to the banks to fund and while they have a specific plan, unfortunately they have a great, insurmountable failure to find liquidity to implement their plan. This should have brought us today to the bill, to make it easier for entrepreneurship to go beyond the aid scheme to have a financial facility to implement its investment project. Unfortunately, this cannot be done. However, the particularly favourable overall framework of support provided through different forms of incentives to strategic and simple private investors could serve as a model for supporting similar smaller-scale investment initiatives at the level of small and medium-sized enterprises. There are also special cases which should be highlighted and predicted in areas affected by major disasters, such as recent natural disasters or fires in Evia and Elias. It could therefore provide for an automatic activation to a higher rate of grant, both for new investments and for those who have suffered damage, which could be even greater if it was linked to a conservation or job creation clause. Now, in the third part, which concerns the establishment and operation of technicians' companies, we believe that the provisions are positive and we will vote for them because we too want to see the companies grow, the “spin-off” companies, since we believe they are one of the healthy and innovative solutions for the development of our economy. After all, we should not underestimate that our country has a highly trained scientific capacity, and we should all contribute to the best possible use of it. Ladies and gentlemen, in the Change Movement we traditionally support, as we continue to support, all kinds of investment initiatives, since they respect the environment and because they are necessary infrastructures contributing to a fair energy transition, but also based on a balanced framework of incentives, both with foreigners and potential investors. And obviously we are not fearful with “fast track” procedures, since we first institutionalised Mr. 3894/2010, speeding up and transparent implementation of strategic investment. Proof of the great interest that we have as a Change Movement for the implementation of strategic investment is the emblematic investment of the Greek, which we have supported from the concept of even today that have begun some support work at the political, social, economic and ideological level in order to compel investment and to implement it. But we can't tell the investments as the government does. We give them the role they really play as a potential, very dynamic development tool. In attracting private sector investment, we believe that competition with the State is not the main aim, nor the demonization of the State, but the complementarity of the two. Known for us must be one of the safeguarding of the public interest and the condition is that transparency and not photographic settings or ribbon “tailor made” of provisions to investors who are still ghosts. However, the government's general policy on the matter, its unexplained obsessions and the quite problematic articles of the bill, which cause the praisers even within the limits of the government's party, shatter whatever good will and exceed our tolerances. The common objective is to make our country a real investment city, of course, while respecting the necessary environmental safety controls, so that sustainable development can be achieved. This objective cannot be achieved without a coherent framework, without rules and stable strategy, which unfortunately, ladies and gentlemen, are absent from today's bill. For the amendments, which have been tabled literally at night, I shall be placed in my dentistry. Thank you.

ParlaMint-GR_2021-11-30-S1-commons.u14	Thank you, Mr Arseni. Before giving the floor to the President of the Greek Parliamentary Group Mr Velopoulos, the Deputy Minister of Education Mr. Syrigos will take the floor for three minutes to develop two provisions of the bill relating to his Ministry. Here, Mr. Syrigos, you have the floor.
ParlaMint-GR_2021-11-30-S1-commons.u15	Thank you very much, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, I will refer to the Third Part of the bill concerning technobic companies and I will start with one specific example. A few days ago, I was visited by a team from the School of Chemical Engineering of Metsovio Polytechnics, which took second place in a European competition for ecological and innovative food products, Ecotrophelia Europe 2021. The children presented a salty cereal bar - all the cereal bars that exist are sweet - based on the village salad and various other cereal bars always based on known Greek flavors. Let's assume, then, that this group or a group of people were going to take advantage of this innovative product they made. The first question, then, is who could be involved in the craft company. Under the existing system, it could only be their teacher. Because today's law requires only those ICP members who have been appointed three years and researchers of research centres. In this case, however, there were postdoctoral students, postgraduate students, who could not participate. The new bill solves this. All these prerequisites are abolished and all those involved in creating knowledge and the results of research, i.e. members of the JEPs, as well as research staff and academic scholars and postdoctoral researchers, are involved. Problem two. How are intellectual property issues regulated by this bar? On the basis of the existing framework, there was no specific arrangement as to who the beneficiary of these intellectual property rights was. Now, under this law exclusive competence is conferred on bodies, which by rules define all aspects of intellectual property. A third problem that would be present today is who they decide. At the moment, on the basis of the legislation in force, the university consortium decides. The Aristotle University is sixty-seven people. Imagine this multi-faceted instrument, which meets once a month, must be decided on a craftsman's company. It changes the framework radically and with the new law it decides on the fewer and more flexible democracies that can meet at any time and so decision-making is facilitated. Another problem that so far existed was the time limit for decision-making on the establishment of a technobic. They took these young people, these young people, the young scientists submitted their proposal, but there was no time limit within which someone had to decide. In the new law the establishment or non-company of a technician shall be issued not later than four months after the application has been made, otherwise it shall be deemed that the application has been approved if there is a positive recommendation from the competent body. Another problem that exists so far is the rejection of the request. To date a request may be rejected without explaining why it is rejected. By the new law, any rejection of the request for the setting up of a technocratic company by the competent bodies must be justified. At the same time, it is also imperative that researchers who have invented the patent move quickly. Today there is no time limit for setting up a craftsman's company, and now a time limit is set, which means that within six months researchers have to decide what to do with their invention. Another major problem which, at least in the committee, was the question of whether the researcher is developing. One brake in the whole procedure was that researchers, especially if they are members of a LPIS, could not evolve if they participated in the Technoblast Company. So when they were going to choose between the two, they usually chose the CPI position. Now they are given the opportunity to develop internally. In this way, then, the researcher's development continues to continue properly. [[At this point, the closing bell of the speaking time of the Minister is ringing.]] Mr President, I would like a little tolerance. Another major problem was how these people, what laboratories use, what the facilities are, what the means they will use. With today's legislation there was incredible ambiguity as to what is happening with these inventions and how the research organisation is related to these inventions. The new law introduces the conclusion of a contract between the research organisation, whether it is a research centre or a research university, and the researchers and the technicians' company and details all aspects of the operation of the technicians, how the offices use them, how laboratories use the machinery, logos, trademarks, everything. And by law there is a minimum percentage of 1% of the technoblast's gross revenues. Last point is that in case these researchers want to cooperate with another research organisation so far there has not been the relevant framework. This law introduces for the first time the participation of researchers from different research bodies. In conclusion, all the issues that have so far been fragmentary and incomplete in five different legislative texts are regulated in a single text and we set the basic conditions for the effective and efficient use of the results of scientific research carried out within the scientific bodies of our country. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-GR_2021-11-30-S1-commons.u18	Thank you, Mr President. The Minister for Development and Investment Mr Dimas has the floor now.
ParlaMint-GR_2021-11-30-S1-commons.u19	Thank you very much, Mr President. I will make a very brief comment. If, in fact, we do love and want to protect our grandparents and grandparents, our fathers and our mothers, as has already been said in the Chamber, the first thing we do is to urge them, with arguments, to go and to be vaccinated. And in a national effort there are not half the words, half the commitments. We demand that all the parliamentary parties support this national campaign, because if we are not vaccinated, then we are all at risk. And that is something we must not forget. On the contrary, we expect a much greater responsibility from the political parties. But now we're going to the issue of the bill. I'll talk about Part III. I would like to tell you that until now there has been no clear institutional framework for technobularies, spin-off research centres and universities. A year and a half ago we started together with the Ministry of Education an effort to see how we can create a clear institutional framework for the operation of universities and research centres for technocrats. We addressed the National Council for Research and Innovation, the ESETEC. After discussions, which lasted for over a year, through the Sectoral Intellectual Property and Technology Transfer Scientific Council, the ESRB has come up with a text in which there was a synthesis of researchers, from ICP members, from marketmen, from many different academic institutions, and we now have a consensus in a very important bill, in an important section of the research organisations for the operation of technocrats. I mention this because I would like to emphasise the importance of public consultation with stakeholders, with those directly involved. We believe it is an attempt, an optimal practice in the part of the legislation. And let me once again thank the Step of the House publicly all and those who helped us get the result today. Why, however, was it necessary to regulate the technicians' companies? In international practice, the craftsmen, spin-offs, are companies established in research organisations to develop and exploit commercial intellectual property or scientific knowledge, produced by the researchers of the research organisation concerned and to develop innovative products and services. In cases where researchers have developed inventions or innovation that has significant market dynamics, technicians companies are the best vehicle to make effective and profitable for researchers and the research organisation the commercial exploitation of their research. In Greece, our researchers are developing significant inventions and are producing international leading scientific knowledge, with significant potential for commercial use on the international market. However, it needs impetus and support so that inventions, research and scientific knowledge are transformed into new products, new services and new benefits for society. That is why it was necessary to strengthen and reform the institutional framework that regulates companies - technobically. Chapter III of the draft law regulates for the first time in a detailed and coherent manner the establishment of the company - technician of research organisations. Although such a central institution for the use of research has remained unregulated to date, with considerable ambiguities, with sporadic provisions, often contradictory, and the institutional framework itself is an obstacle to their development. Neither was terminology clear, as different terms were used in legislation at times, such as, in particular, the term "intensive - knowledge company". Clear or incomplete institutional framework for an institution, means that in practice it will never be possible for companies - artificial ones - to be created and developed. The unclear institutional framework has so far led to malfunctions or misuse of the institution. These, precisely, pathogens are cured by chapter III of the bill. The challenges in creating this new institutional framework were important. Firstly, to date ambiguity about the institution itself. It is precisely because of the unambiguous arrangements, the shortages and the different deadlines that were not common sense and the starting point for everyone as to what it is intended and how a company - a technobetic one - is organised. Secondly, the specificities of the individual scientific fields and the different conditions for exploiting research by field, such as differences between life sciences and information technology in terms of the maturity of the research and the cost of product development. Flexible arrangements are needed to ensure that crucial elements of the establishment and operation of the company, for example in assessing the intellectual property exploited and the price of rights paid by the company, are regulated on a case-by-case basis to take account of the specificities of the scientific fields. Thirdly, the risk of the creation of viable companies. A technician, if sustainable, can be an effective and profitable vehicle so that the research can pass through the laboratory on to the market. However, in order to operate effectively and efficiently a technology company, such as any company producing innovation, a commercial risk assessment, a market analysis, a detailed business plan and a significant investment in economic resources and human time is required. The decision to set up a company must therefore have taken these requirements into account. The institutional framework is required to provide for procedures and set conditions for business planning and for the appropriate investment of resources and human-time researchers in the companies to be established. After all, only if the companies - technicians have the fundamental prospects of sustainability will the institutional purpose for which they are founded be achieved. Fourthly, the different way of organizing, but also the different degrees of readiness and maturity of research organisations to carry out research activities. The procedure for establishing and content of the relevant decisions and legal acts - contracts cannot be uniform for all bodies. It is necessary to allow research organisations to provide for more specific or different arrangements on certain issues. On the other hand, we need a weighting of flexicurity with legal security. For the company’s viability on a number of critical issues, it is necessary when approving its establishment or its legal relationship with the organisation that they have been clearly regulated, for example, under the terms of intellectual property rights. The company’s investors and associates require legal certainty. The institutional framework should, in general, contribute to legal security covering incomplete and vague instruments of approval or contracts with the Agency by law. This draft law sets out in detail and clear terms all the key parameters for the establishment and operation of technocrat companies. In particular. Firstly, establishing clearly and precisely which companies will be companies - technoblastists by resolving ambiguities over years, because to date it is not clear or what purpose this institution serves. The institution is now determined on the basis of the status of founders and their business object. Let me say that this is where we have also taken note of the comments from the political parties of the Civilian Representation. We've further specified the definition. I read: ‘Spin-off is now designated as a capital company established by academic or research staff of the country's research organisations for the purpose of exploiting the research results and knowledge produced by the individual on his own or in cooperation with third parties, natural or legal persons of the national or foreign state in the context of his or her activity and with the research organisations’. This is added as a legislative improvement at the end of Article 52 of the plan, i.e. after the case g. the case is added, so we define exactly as we have said, the craftsman. Secondly, putting the same researchers who have developed the research to be exploited at the forefront, defining their rights and responsibility, as well as the conflicts of interest that they have to avoid. The law introduces specific rules to avoid conflicts of interest and recognises the need to regulate them. However, it is a question that the research organisation will in particular regulate in its regulation the cases of conflicts of interest and the obligations to avoid them. Thirdly, by identifying the role of the research organisation, the approvals it needs to grant, the discretion, but not the obligation to participate, and the rights and benefits granted to the company. Fourthly, by clarifying the procedure for approving the establishment of a company by researchers, by simplifying the presentations of the procedure already applied to certain agencies, an approval decision instead of more, clarification of responsibilities and more. Fifthly, by introducing the institution of the contract of techno-blast for clear and detailed regulation of the relationship between the organisation and the company through which, among other things, the exploitation of each intellectual property and knowledge developed in the organisation will be granted. But this is where I want to stand. The establishment of the contract is of crucial importance both for the protection of the public interest of the research organisation, but also for the company's particular private interests, in particular for legal security against third parties. For the research organisation, the contract is a critical institution, because it will define the exchange of the techno-blast towards the research organisation and also the other rights and obligations of the organisation towards the techno-blasting company. The institution of the contract of craftsman is, in essence, regulated and the financial participation of the organisation in the company. Economic participation is not necessary in the form of acquisition of shares or shares, but may be rootalties. [[At this point is the end of the speaker's speaking time bell.]] I'm gonna need some time, Mr. President. A craftsman's contract shall determine the price of rights of exploitation for intellectual property and scientific knowledge and any other exchanges for the benefits of the organisation. For the company, the contract is a critical institution, because it creates legal security against potential investors, associates, customers, as it is clear from the establishment of the company, what commitments the technician has over the organisation and what benefits he receives, however. The term of the contract, defined as up to 10 years, also marks the duration of the contract for the company. With the termination or termination of the contract, the property of the craftsman for the company is also withdrawn and its application to the law and regulations governing the company and the participating researchers is therefore withdrawn. It is important to point out that the law obviously recognises the possibility of an early solution to the contract. The property of the technician may end early for the company if there is disagreement or any other reason provided for in the contract. However, the draft law also sets out a minimum necessary content of the contract, which includes the licensing or transfer of intellectual property rights or the transfer of rights to research results and knowledge, the price of exploitation rights and how it is paid, the concession of use of facilities, the concession of use of organisation and company, information obligations, confidentiality obligations, conflict of interest commitments, conditions of early solution. Sixth, providing for the possibility and conditions of participation of investors in the share capital of the same company. Seventhly, covering regulatory possible deficiencies or negligence in terms of approval decisions and contracts in cases of incomplete regulation by the research organisations themselves to prevent any legal uncertainty and to achieve safety. But there is also the intellectual property research provision. Allow me here and here to stress that the establishment of craftsmen's companies requires clarity and legal certainty regarding the intellectual property regime and, in particular, patents, so-called patents. The law on this matter refers to the regulations of the bodies which have regulated it. However, it clarifies the scheme for those organisations that have not regulated it and stipulates that if the regulation does not specify it, then the inventions of researchers are considered to be service inventions and therefore the full beneficiary of the relevant intellectual property patents is the research organisation with an obligation to return benefits to the researcher. Eighth, it is unclear to date legislation on research and marketing. Researchers can now participate as founders and managers of technobic companies. Artificial founders, partners may be university professors, research centres researchers, research staff or academic staff of each grade, doctoral and postdoctoral researchers. The term ‘researcher’ in Article 52 of the Act is defined in the broadest possible sense and covers the entire range of persons carrying out research within a research organisation, enabling research and academic staff of each grade to establish a technician company. The range of research that can be exploited in business is widened, but the range of those who can take the initiative or even lead to a commercial use effort is widened. It therefore opens the way for young researchers to choose the path of business use of research after their first research steps and to give new dynamics to the research organisation and new prospects in their own future. The way to exploit doctoral and post-doctoral research through technocrat companies is opened. However, the question is also finally being dealt with for companies that are the object of many research organisations. As regards the terms of participation of researchers in the company, Article 57 of the Law resolves institutional ambiguities of years, gives incentives and facilitates institutional researchers. Mostly it acknowledges that a researcher may be not only a partner but also an executive member. In particular, he may be a researcher as a partner, be a researcher as an executive management member or be employed by the researcher in the technician's company with a fee. The law confirms the notion that the researcher does not only carry out research into research, but that it is equally crucial to exploiting research into the economy for society. In conclusion, technicians do not constitute a business vehicle parallel to the research activity but develop and complement academic, research activity. They give a way out of the intellectual property that the researcher develops for the research organisation. It is for this reason that the support for the company's work is part of the job of a researcher. It is laid down that the approval decision establishing the company will provide that the investigator may devote part of his term of office to the support of the technoblaster without having to obtain permission from the conduct of his duties.

Until now ERT has only been able to award such contracts in connection with the production and presentation of a broadcasting programme. The extension of this possibility to other services, such as marketing, commercial exploitation, to social media, is - I think - necessary and self-evident. Why? Because we have all been following up over the last two and a half years in the ERT to upgrade the product produced, the broadcasting programme. The intensity of this program is designed through the acquisition of new rights for both information and entertainment programs such as Greek series, mythoplasia, documentaries and foreign productions. Alongside the traditional instruments, the ERTFLIX digital platform is also developed and established through a wide range of options. So now that the content of ERT has been enriched and it is still evolving, we all realise that there is a need for qualified partners who can support the appropriate projection, so that this object can be widely exploited in order to increase the company's revenues and invest in similar content. The first samples from the user response - I think that everyone agrees on this and, above all, the viewers - are very positive for the changes and for the programme and for what ERT invests and manages. I think we need to enable her to award project contracts other than its traditional methods and for these new needs that competition has created, but also its own infiltration into new objects. In this way, public service broadcasting will be even more capable of playing a leading role in competition, which is necessary for ERT to play the role that we all want. The other articles in the amendment, Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, concern the legislative improvements made to the President-in-Office's Decree 261 of '97, which in many ways governs the way in which State advertising is carried out, as is the case to date and what we are improving and how we regulate. In accordance with the applicable rules and the proposed improvements, public advertising expenditure is divided into two parts: The first section concerns events, creatively, media plan development, service support in all of the above and is carried out in compliance with the procedures of the n. This Regulation shall enter into force on the twentieth day following that of its publication in the Official Journal of the European Union. The second section relates to the assignment of the transmission and registration of messages, that is to say the entry of messages to the media, carried out on the basis of a specific invoice, in accordance with the current Presidential Decree. As we all understand, this delegation is not subject to a separate competitive procedure. It is made on the basis of the framework drawn up by the contractor resulting from the competitive procedure that I mentioned before. It is quite distinct and does not fall within the scope of the n. Date of birth: 4412/2016. I would like to point out that on the basis of the Presidential Decree currently in force - this Presidential Decree has been in force since 1997 - and in particular Articles 6 and 7 thereof, the State, the contracting authorities, namely the Ministries, has been able to conclude direct contracts with the Media for the promotion and registration of the advertising message. It is not legally clear enough and it is not clear how the contracting authorities, i.e. the Ministries, will be able to pay the contractors. What do we come and do, then, now with the proposed arrangement? The contracting authorities, i.e. the Ministries, are given the opportunity to pay directly to the Media of Mass Information for the promotion or registration - again - of the advertising message, on the basis of always the binding plan, the media plan, which has been submitted by the contractor who has come from the competitive process that I have already described to you under n. Date of birth: 4412/2016. This is how the following benefits arise, Mr President, ladies and gentlemen. First of all, it is clear that it is a contractual relationship between the contracting authority, i.e. the Ministry, and the Mass Information Instrument, which will show the message or record by the contractor who will display the message or register it. Secondly, there is a avoidance of any conflict of interest, since otherwise the one who made the framework would also be the one who would pay for registration or promotion in the media. Thirdly, transparency is even greater, as we complete the provisions of the Presidential Decree - they were strong, but we complement them even more - and it is foreseen that in order to pay the contracting authorities, namely the Ministries, the media plan should provide all the necessary evidence, the orders, the evidence of visibility to the contracting authorities, that is to say to the Ministries. Fourthly, given that the payment money will now be managed by the contracting authorities, i.e. the Greek State, the Ministries, and not the contractors, it is essentially limited to the possibility of non-transparent transactions or commercial practices, i.e. reimbursements for those who know how this market is moving, between the Mass Information Instruments and the contractors who compile the media plan. Fifthly, because, as a general rule, the expenditure on the promotion and registration of these messages in the media is much higher than the rest of the advertising services undertaken by the contractors and which is the product of the competitive procedures and since they will be paid directly by the public bodies, always on the basis of - I repeat - the project that will be submitted to the Ministries by the contractor resulting from the competitive process, the possibility is that we do not lose much time and the promotion of the messages of the State should be done in time and quickly and the rest of the competitive procedure for the contractor should be much shorter. Finally, and also important. It is imported into the Presidential Decree - there was a blurred provision - authorizing the provision of any necessary detail on the forming of the price list and the final prices offered to the State, to the public bodies, to the media, to release these prices from the Ministry responsible to all public bodies carrying out public advertising and to this end, transparency is enhanced and more competitive the distribution of the services concerned. For reasons of legal security and speed and additional transparency, this arrangement also takes over outstanding payments of 2021. The tabling of the amendment at this time was considered necessary so that we can proceed with the necessary procedures without the problems, perhaps the bureaucracy and the ambiguities that existed in the previous period, so that we can immediately and quickly run a television campaign to inform the Greek public of the need to extend the vaccination coverage to both the first and third doses. Thank you.

ParlaMint-GR_2021-11-30-S1-commons.u78	Thank you, Mr President. I will be placed for the third part in terms of creating a framework for companies that are technicians in higher education institutions and research centres. All other parties 'yes, but' etc. This new institutional framework, which deals with the bill, is clearly aimed at establishing innovative capital companies to promote a more flexible way of interconnecting and transferring research results from research institutions to the capitalist market rather than using research and science for the benefit of social needs. This objective is served by this bill, and the JRC is obviously against it. The need to clarify what companies are artificial, their subject matter, their process of establishment is done by the bill because government and private capital are interested not in the risk posed by the entrepreneurialisation of public research institutions, but this is their objective - but because they are in a hurry to implement the basic direction of the bourgeois order for the development of innovation ecosystems, which is recognised as one of the main priorities for the commercialisation of the research process. Besides, his development law is n. 4399/2016, previously in PASOK, linked the investigation to the capitalist market. Obviously, many of the ideas on which these companies are being set up may be innovative, useful and necessary. However, most of the time, they are rejected because they are not necessary or profitable for capital and the market at any given time, so that the direction of research and academic studies is directed towards and dictated by the trends and interests of businesses and the market, instead of being carried out with an incentive to the needs, their utility and the development of technology. Is the example of Covid -19 where in previous years the study of SARS and MERS was stopped, why was it not profitable? While now a large number of companies are racing in the field of competition for what will be the first to find the vaccine or appropriate treatment. The account that the establishment of technicians' companies is an opportunity for young researchers, even as a solution to the underemployment of the highly skilled workforce or as a factor in contributing to the development of the regional and national economy, because 'all together can', because 'capitalist development is for everyone' and other such physiologies, are intended to pass on to the consciousness of the young people who have been given the opportunity to research and their object of helping to overcome the crisis. Of course, if capitalist growth is for everyone, it turns out to be daily and, above all, the experience of the previous crisis and of course, of the capitalist crisis. It is the flagship example of tourism, the'motor of the economy', as you say, with workers and micro-enterprises being crushed and the middle-flooders coming out won. You are actually mocking thousands of young people who are trying to win their future, but they are facing unemployment, insecurity, flexible working relationships, competition, who will first get a research result, if this is financed by a company, if the intellectual and economic benefits of their work are fruitful, if the small company that set up their own money or their family at some point will pay and gain the interest of a sponsor. You sell them algae for silk ribbons that my best chances will stop brain escape abroad, the well-known Brain drain and the achievement of Brain gain will come. Nevertheless, how a young man will succeed in setting up such a company, what life expectancy he will have, if he will keep his intellectual rights, how many jobs he opens, what working conditions he will work for, for all the above, the supporters of the antidote are indifferently whistling at Brain Drain. It is obvious that trying to ideologically manipulate young scientists, trying to put them on the weight of the way out of their own capitalist economic crisis, discourage them and disappoint them their dreams for a better life. The formation of 'innovation hubs' - so called - hosting groups of young people by developing a start-up entrepreneurial idea, make clear the direction of research in sectors that generate profit, the criterion of which is the entrepreneurial benefit and the commercialisation of its results. The large capital is evident to avoid taking the investment risk for the development of all new knowledge and the high cost of innovation production and transfer it to the university, to the research centres. In practice, the university gambles reserves and funds on behalf of large enterprises which should be intended to meet the needs of teaching, research and educational needs of their students. The possibility of granting intellectual property rights by research institutions, the use of spaces and infrastructures for consideration, renting for immovable property, the use of the university's name to companies, objectively intensifys competition, categorisation among the institutions and will be linked to the so-called assessment of teachers on market criteria. In other words, the teacher-manager with business links will be better assessed by his colleagues who give the necessary weight to the process of education and much more. The university's operation will therefore become more and more merchandise. We, as JRC, stand against linking entrepreneurship to the educational process, the operation of schools, universities, research centres. It undermines the creativity of the researcher, the free perception, the free exchange of views among scientists, with a view to the intellectual production of new scientific knowledge for the benefit of social well-being. This is why the JRC claims only public funding at the level of real needs. We are fighting for the solution of the objections that capitalism brings to the educational and research process. Within the framework of the requirement to abolish all business activities in universities, the JRC also claims to abolish the EPPs which are leveraging the university and research community. Entrepreneurship cannot solve the problem of distance but within the possibilities of developing science and research and exploiting its results from society. In our view, entrepreneurship is the reason why it kept the universities closed up for more than a year and underscore the educational function. If you ask us, though, if innovation is bad, of course it is not bad. For the JRC the connection of new knowledge to production is done and will always be made. It is necessary to introduce new knowledge into production. But we are not generally talking about knowledge about knowledge, but about knowledge for the benefit of social needs. Certainly the above cannot be done as long as the means of production are capitalist property and the purpose of production is profit. So, in the end, research can only be subject to this purpose. ParlaMint-GR_2021-11-30-S1-commons.u105	And we thank the Minister. The speaker of the New Democracy, Mr. Xenophilas Baraliakos, has the floor for seven minutes.
ParlaMint-GR_2021-11-30-S1-commons.u106	Thank you very much, Mr President. Minister, ladies and gentlemen, this draft law by the Ministry of Development and Investment attempts to establish rules aimed at improving the investment environment in our country, speeding up and flexible the administrative procedures envisaged, for the strategies and private investment. At the same time, it promotes systemicisation in a single legislative framework of the way in which a technocratic company, known as spin-off, is set up and run, with the main aim of simplifying procedures, removing ambiguities identified in the existing legislative framework and modernising it. So starting with the strategic investment in investment in international economic activities of marketable products and services, the legal framework is amended to join the category of strategic investment and new modern sectors of our economy. For example, agri-food, biotechnology, waste and waste management, digital reshaping of businesses, robotics and artificial intelligence, providing computing clouds, data centres and business parks. The framework for the budget is also amended, which is now reduced to qualifying for an investment as a strategy, taking into account combined factors such as the number of job-creating young people, but also whether the investment relates to new, modern sectors. At the same time, incentives are provided for strategic investment, such as incentives for placement, rapid licensing incentives, fast track procedures, tax incentives, capital incentives and tax residence. A key condition in the process of evaluating such an investment is, as clearly described in the draft law, that jobs are created with emphasis on skilled personnel. Strategic investments can produce quantitative and qualitative results of significant intensity to increase employment. With this draft law we show our intention to move even stronger in this direction, continuing to fulfil the election commitment and vision of our Prime Minister, Mr.Mitsotakis, for many and good jobs and more specifically for the qualified staff to create the conditions that will provide opportunities, but also for the high-trained scientific potential that we have and will reverse the painful phenomenon of our country of Brain Drain, bringing our children back to their homes, back to a Greece that is changing. In the case of private investment, the possibility of placing new activities is strengthened and accelerated, for which there is a strong investment interest. In particular, Articles relating to the competent bodies for receiving applications for investment projects are amended, the documentation and evaluation process, the control and monitoring of implementation and how the approved aid and benefits are paid. For investments of over EUR 1,000 000, the Directorate-General for Private Investment of the Ministry of Development and Investment is now responsible. The evaluation stage includes sworn auditors, who are already involved in the audit phase of investment projects under previous development laws, and an important instrument is established that sets out to a series the investment projects of all stakeholders, which is no other than the time of the administrative decisions on the certification of interim phases of implementation of investment projects that may not exceed 30 days from the submission of the relevant audit reports or attestations. In that regard, the Register of Observers of Accountants is hereby established. Finally, the framework is created for companies - technicians, the wider known as spin-off, which are companies that have as their object the commercial and business exploitation of the research results and their direct interconnection with the real economy in our country and are, at last, established by members of academic or research staff of the research organisations. Investigators shall be entitled to participate as executive members of the technician, to be employed in him by a project contract and to provide research or advisory support. Techniques can be contractors and in such a case they are treated like any other private company. The draft law cures, with the present regulations, the shortcomings in the spin-off companies, such as the lack of technology transfer offices through the ESRB call and the lack of funding to a very large extent through the financial instruments of the Investment Fund of Entrepreneurial Participation, which since 2018, as well as through the actions of the ESRB, such as "Research - I Create Innovation" and other technological maturation. The proposed arrangements codified in a single legislative framework the way in which a technology company is set up and operated, with the main aim of opening wide open the way to investors who intend to invest in these companies, from the development of which, taking into account the investment interest in these companies in our country and, more generally, in the newly-born businesses, benefit the Greek economy by increasing employment and strengthening prosperity. Finally, why not multiply now that there will be a framework that is company-friendly at home for the first time. Ladies and gentlemen, the draft law incorporates the business future of our country with more jobs, especially for our young people, but also a comprehensive business and economic development for the whole country. It is a great bet that will improve the lives of Greek citizens. The direction of the Ministry of Development and Investment is stable, clear and identical to the new global business reality. We build a modern, thriving and competitive Greece that will reverse the Brain drain in Brain gain, a Greece that gives prospects to each Greek and every Greek and will keep our children active in the productive mechanism of our country. So I call on you to vote on this draft law and all to join together in this great leap that we are taking towards development. Thank you.

ParlaMint-GR_2021-11-30-S1-commons.u121	Thank you, Mr. Bukowo, too. I shall now give the floor to the Secretary-General responsible, Mr Vésyropoulos, to put him on the amendment under general number 1146 and specialist number 93.
ParlaMint-GR_2021-11-30-S1-commons.u122	Thank you, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, I take the floor to refer to the amendment with a general number of 1146 and with a special number 93 tabled by the Ministry of Finance. This is an amendment with articles which, among other things, modernise and rationalise tax legislation and which come to be voted on in the framework of a bill aimed at creating a modern and attractive environment, suitable for attracting and supporting the implementation of strategic investment. In particular, Article 8 of the amendment harmonises tax incentives for the development and exploitation of patents in Article 71A of the Code of Income Tax with the rules of the Organisation for Economic Cooperation and Development to avoid formal tax practices. To date the undertakings made use of the favourable arrangements provided for in Article 71a for exemption from the income tax on patented profits, irrespective of whether they had incurred costs for the creation, development or improvement of that patent. Article 8 of the amendment changes and hence the exemption from tax is linked to the research and development costs incurred by the company for the development of patents. At the same time, the scope of Article 71a is extended so that other revenues relating to the exploitation of the patent may be taken into account from the sale of the production products of the undertaking and from the provision of services for which patents were used, such as the proceeds from the sale of patents. This is a regulation perfectly reasonable which incorporates international rules and practices, modernising our tax framework and which should therefore be a broad agreement and consensus. Article 9 of the amendment lays down ceilings on the fines imposed in the tax procedure for cases where an undertaking does not produce its books under tax control, as well as on tax electronics, memories and records created by tax electronics. This is a streamlining of the provisions of Article 54e of the Code of Conduct on account of the activation of the MyDATA Digital Platform and the obligation to transmit the documentary data to the independent public revenue authority. This regulation sets a ceiling on fines of 15% of the company's gross revenue. In addition, it provides that the fine is imposed if, during the period of preventive tax control, no tax electronic mechanisms or also records and memories of tax electronic mechanisms are produced unless a loss has been declared before the relevant control order is issued. The regulation in Article 10 of the amendment follows Article 9 in order to make it clear that in the event of a breach or manipulation or interference in the operation of tax electronics, where the fine provided for in Article 54e of the Tax Code does not apply, the maximum limitation of the fine shall not apply. Such clarification shall be necessary in view of the particular gravity of the infringement. Ladies and gentlemen, without doubt this amendment brought to the vote by the Government of Kyriakos Mitsotakis rationalises and updates the tax framework in the light of technological developments in digital tax data transmission to the tax administration, but also incorporates international tax practices and should receive broad cross-party support. Thank you.

ParlaMint-GR_2021-11-30-S1-commons.u175	Come on, Mr. Dermetzopoulou.
ParlaMint-GR_2021-11-30-S1-commons.u176	Thank you, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, the debate before the national delegation today concerns an innovative draft law on Greek data. A bill that comes to regulate the scope of strategic investment and the improvement of the investment environment by speeding up procedures for private and strategic investment and creating a framework for technocratic companies. But why now is such a bill? In the midst of a pandemic, our scientific community introduced innovative ideas, new plans, trade proposals, but without being able either to transform them into acts or to legally safeguard them for future use. Researchers in Greece are developing considerable inventions and leading internationally to their research activity. In view of the support of the research community to exploit these results it produces, the institutional framework that regulates the establishment and operation of spin offs companies is reformed. It is true that in recent years the Greek state had an incomplete regulatory framework which contained unclear and contradictory provisions. This was a dissuasive element for research bodies and organisations to make practical use of the research they produced. With the new law, therefore, researchers have a key role to play in the development of an investment opportunity presented to them, they acquire clear rights and prioritise the development and development of their work. In practice, for a spin off company to function effectively, a sound and prudent commercial risk assessment is required, a market analysis, a weighting of commercial demand and supply factors, a detailed business plan, as well as a significant investment in economic and human resources. The economic success of the use of this mechanism depends on the choice of the most appropriate method of use which should be based on the purpose of the research organisation, the type of transfer of technology and the financial resources available to the research organisation. The specific objectives of the spin off-companies are the economic exploitation of existing research results by carrying out research projects through the creation of new companies. Intensification of research and similar activities in private and public enterprises and research bodies. Promoting entrepreneurship in education and research institutions. The increase in employment in particular in knowledge sectors. The creation of a financial framework for initial support for projects. The extension of cooperation with business and research organisations abroad and their integration into international permanent or specific cooperation networks. For the success of the project, however, the creation of a framework for these companies is a presumption that regulations are needed to improve the investment environment in our country and to speed up and simplify the administrative procedures for strategic and private investment. This draft law is structured in three interrelated parties. The first concerns strategic investments aimed at reforming and improving the existing regulatory framework by treating any unclear elements and provisions on strategic investment. The second part of the bill refers to private investment and the aim is to speed up the procedures for granting aid. In this way, substantial improvements are being made in the process of assessing and controlling investments in order to make them effective. The third part lists the provisions and conditions for the establishment of spin off companies. Ladies and gentlemen, the development of the trade idea in Greece in recent years has been based on fragmentary legislation and many ambiguities that discouraged the candidate investor. In recent years the business had been demonized as an automation, thus being treated. Conditions that favour investment attraction and activity are, together with others, mutual trust, honest behavior between the world of entrepreneurship and the state, the absence of all sorts of bureaucracy, enhanced digital function, political stability and clear terms on either side. And these conditions, in practice, are now to be seen that only the Government of Mr.Mitsotakis and himself is to ensure them, and this is abstractly demonstrated by the fact that our country has become globally renowned as one of the most attractive investment destinations. This is why the Government of New Democracy, loyal to its vision of innovative investment and multiple development policy, today brings this draft law, which will create a new air at a standstill in the expansion of knowledge and innovation. By simplifying the procedures for the establishment and development of the spin-off companies, we modernise their operating environment and set new foundations for the development of entrepreneurship. We are therefore giving solutions to a problem which has been a yearly problem and which has limited the possibility of exploiting domestic research produced. We are therefore strengthening research and linking it even more effectively with innovation and entrepreneurship. If we do not do it now, so that we are competitive on a global level, then we will only have to be overcome by developments and we must only look ahead. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-GR_2021-11-30-S1-commons.u202	Thank you, Mr President. Minister, ladies and gentlemen, the bill we are debating today helps speed up procedures and creates new categories of strategic investments adapted to the development model of the next day. It provides new incentives for the implementation and promotion of strategic investment in our country. It consolidates and improves the existing institutional framework for strategic investments, as established by Laws 4608/2019 and 3894/2010. The draft law also makes important changes to the development law, particularly in the direction of speeding up the evaluation procedures for investment projects. There is no doubt that people in the market and the economy appreciate and appreciate the provisions of the bill positively. At this juncture, a sterile opposition to the opposition is under way, especially when everyone is aware that wealth growth and production are not coming through a state-owned economy but through capital that is mobilising and investing in private initiative. The area of strategic investment is linked to fast track procedures, overcoming obstacles that have so far acted as a burden to implement investments. Writing impulse of responsibilities, time-consuming procedures in particular on complex issues, such as spatial, urban and environmental issues. From day one, the government has been moving to strengthen the institutional framework for strategic investment. In fact, three of the most important changes that took place and which were incorporated into Laws 4635/2019 and 4712/20 had been discussed together with the Minister of Development, Mr.Georgiadis and his legal advisers and touched many future strategic investments and the great investment that is being prepared in my place of Lefkada for the production of Marina in Vlychos. The most important provision was tabled and voted in October 2019 and reduced the budget in order to qualify as an investment at EUR 40 million, of the 100 million that was the threshold, while creating seventy-five new jobs. In this way it has been possible to join strategic investments and the marina in Vlychos of Lefkada, which is in fact the first municipal investment ever approved. Ladies and gentlemen, in addition to the incentives for rapid placement and licensing, additional incentives are provided with a fixed tax framework over a period of 12 years but also with specific tax exemptions, such as an income tax exemption on pre-tax profits. These incentives are further strengthened since the provisions of the bill provide for capital aid, the subsidy of the leasing and the subsidy of the wage costs of new jobs and recruitment. At the same time, a new realistic and, above all, targeted classification of strategic investments in five categories is being created. ‘Medical investment 1’ means a budget of more than EUR 75 million or EUR 40 million provided that seventy-five new jobs are created. ‘Medical investment 2’ means investments of EUR 20 million in sectors with multiplier effects such as agri-food, research, biotechnology, cultural and creative industries, robotics, artificial intelligence, internal tourism, waste and waste management and space industry. Alternatively in this category, more than EUR 30 million could be invested with the obligation to create fifty new jobs or 20 million with forty new jobs. "Experienced investments" linked to the green economy and energy. Such investments may be reconnected to the Recovery Fund and receive tax and capital incentives provided that the investment has been carried out by 31 December 2025. ‘Rapid licensing strategies’ linked to the 30 new jobs criterion with a budget of more than EUR 20 million or an investment of EUR 10 million with thirty new jobs in a rated strategic investment. Investments with a hundred new jobs with an investment in a budget of more than EUR 15 million for the modernisation of installations are included in the same category. ‘Equitable integrated strategic investments’ relating to PPP projects and European energy projects of common interest or to the development of business parks. Particularly important are the provisions to make development law regimes more attractive, which is achieved by new more flexible and faster procedures for processing control and supervision of an investment. In fact, the audit procedure will now be carried out by a auditor but also by a certified private body and by an expert in the register of certified assessors. In addition, for the first time, a temporary residence permit is granted to foreign companies to prepare for their activities. Finally, a major innovation in the bill is the arrangements for the establishment and operation of companies of technicians most known as spin off to exploit the research. For the first time, the possibility of interconnecting research with entrepreneurship for the production and promotion of innovative products and services, and also by research staff from public universities and research centres, is being encouraged. Ladies and gentlemen, the vote on this bill and on all the reforming institutional interventions of this government creates the preconditions for another Greece. I would like to congratulate the Minister of Development, Mr Georgiadis, the Deputy Minister, Mr. Nikos Papathanassis, the Deputy Minister, Christos Dimas and the Secretary-General and their staff for another bill with a strong growth and investment mark. The result of these interventions is that in 2021 investments increased 12.9%, while the forecast for 2022 is 15.1%. By improving legislation, we are motivating through faster placement and licensing, tax and capital incentives. This is how we form a pro-investment environment, which mobilizes private capital. This environment, together with the reduction of taxes and measures with a social focus, which increase the available income, creates all the conditions for a new Greece, Greece of growth, progress, wealth production, Greece of new and well-paid jobs, the creative Greece that we deserve. Thank you very much.
