We all know areas that are worldwide known for their achievements in the field of technology and innovation, Silicon Valley in California, Tel Aviv in Israel. These areas act as a steam engine for the economic development of the wider region to which they belong. In order not to speak abstractly but to connect it to our country, I will take an example from Israel, the University of Jerusalem. This university has about the size of the research and academic ecosystem of Crete, as far as researchers and students are concerned. The technology transfer company at the University of Jerusalem, YISSUM, has founded a hundred seventy spin-offs, whose products generate sales of about $2 billion a year. The total economy created by the University of Jerusalem amounts to $3 billion or €2.7 billion. And because I said that this university has the size of the research institutes in Crete, the figure of 3 billion is about 30% of Crete's GDP. So what's going on in Greece? How can we go in this direction? The research carried out at the Greek universities is of high quality and recognized by international bodies. From universities come the majority, about 80%, of the research project and when rankings are based on research results, our universities are classified fairly high. We have won the tenth place in Europe in an absolute number of research programmes - I note in absolute number - of the European Union at HORIZON 2020. Greece had about three thousand Greek participants in a thousand and twenty-five projects and we exceeded many countries with a significantly larger population than our country. Publication and research work also have a high level. The resonance, i.e. the recognition of the work by foreigners is much higher than the European average but also the average of the OECD countries. At the same time, however, while taking the tenth place in Europe in research, according to the European Commission's reports, we are twenty-five in innovation. So we need to bridge this gap between research and knowledge transfer into the real economy. That's why we need to move on three axes. First axle: institutional framework. This draft law establishes a modern and comprehensive framework for technobularies, spin-offs, compared to the previous one, which was incomplete and fragmented. We have facilitated the procedures for establishing intellectual property rights, an area in which we are far shortened, despite the high level and quality of research. It facilitates the dissemination of research results on the market through technology transfer contracts for the concession of intellectual property rights. We have the establishment of spin-offs, technicians, that is, new innovative businesses from universities, and participation of universities in them, with the aim of exploiting research results and facilitating the participation of researchers in the academic community. We have raised considerable revenues at the university by transferring technology but also contributing to the country's productive reconstruction and the creation of new, quality jobs throughout the economy. This has two-way benefits both in businesses and in universities and research institutions. Businesses will offer universities, not only additional financial resources, but also a range of real problems seeking solutions, the challenges for innovative research and the production of new knowledge and the possibility of stepping up laboratory research and approaching the commercial use of results. The university, on the other hand, will provide business with access to new knowledge and innovative methods for localised problems and the emergence of malfunctions but also financially, commercial benefits in the value chain. The second axis concerns entrepreneurship culture. Let me refer to a personal experience at this point. I have already participated in the establishment of spin-offs in both the United States and our country. In 2013, I applied to elect a dean at my university, the University of Crete. During my speech to the university community, in the context of the electoral process, I was attacked by some allegedly progressive, how I, a man of business, dared to run for dean. I responded calmly to them that the university's interconnection with the economy was on my top priorities and I wished that when my term of office was over, the companies that operate around the foundation have multiplyed. Today two of the most successful start - ups in Greece have been founded by graduates of the Polytechnic University of Crete. Today, as was said at the meeting of the Parliament's Standing Committee on Formats Affairs, which held a series of meetings on the interconnection of universities with the economy, entrepreneurship and innovation are not taboo at our university. Our young people are looking for innovation. Many successful examples start - ups from young graduates are a fact. It's in front of us. So we come to fill the gap and make it easier for them to do what they want to do. Thirdly, support for research bodies is needed to develop technology transfer structures. We are using the available funds, tools, the NRP, the Recovery Fund so that our research bodies can quickly cover the lost ground, technology transfer offices can support technology transfer, through the development of a concrete strategy and integrated support mechanism from each university for the creation of technoblasts, with advisory services, networking, provision and finding funding, clarification of intellectual property status, with the maturation of research results and business plans. Ladies and gentlemen, the innovation map in the Greek research ecosystem changes, changes quickly. It is shown by the confidence of large global companies in the Greek research institutions. We are for the first time optimistic that the Greek university can finally fulfil its third purpose, which is to transfer knowledge to the economy for the benefit of society. Thank you.

ParlaMint-GR_2021-11-30-S1-commons.u231	We are now continuing with Mr Tarantili Christos from New Democracy.
ParlaMint-GR_2021-11-30-S1-commons.u232	Minister, ladies and gentlemen, the primary objective of this draft law is the creation of a single legislative framework for strategic and private investment, as we have said, as well as the first systeming of the rules governing the establishment and operation of technicians' companies, spin-offs. The regulatory spectrum of the proposed provisions shows that the government intends to lay the institutional foundations for a solid and long-term investment perspective for the country, which will lead to sustainable development. The provisions we are about to vote on today are a transition to a modern investment strategy, which follows international investment trends and needs, so that our country will become a attraction, both at the level of attracting new activities and at the level of administrative adequacy of all kinds of investment projects. This bill extends the scope of eligible strategic investments to boost strategic investment. Incentives shall be updated for those investments that occur in emblems for the country. The procedures for the integration of private investment, which are covered by aid schemes, are being speeded up for the first time by a legislative framework which will make a decisive contribution to the business use of research, namely the production of innovative products and services at public universities and research centres, with a view to addressing the major international markets. This draft attempts structural interventions in the above areas at a time when the implementation of the National Recovery Plan starts and Greece recovers its position as a credible investment destination. The adoption of the proposed provisions is decisive if we ultimately want to turn our eyes on the future and on the roads of extroversion and innovation, or if we want the opportunities offered by the international context to leave them unexploited. Our country needs to take advantage of its competitive advantages and consolidate its position as a key investment destination in the wider region, which will give it development impetus and guarantee the well-being of citizens in the years to come. With this data I ask you to vote on the proposed draft, to give the signal that Greece wants and can welcome investments that are both modern and sustainable and strategies, to strengthen the private investment that is the backbone of national economy and regional development and to give a vote of confidence in the capacities of public research and public institutions, so that a valuable capital can become a strong growth advantage for our country. Thank you very much.
